    ISAR's publication Surviving Together: A Journal on                                      Elizabeth Clyma
Soviet- American Relations chronicles the many and diverse                                   Jonathan Elkind
activities in Soviet-American relations. It reports on current                               Bridget Murphy
                                                                                              RuthM. Saxe
events in the Soviet Union, exchanges, joint projects, trade,                              Karen A. Shoopman
legislation, public education programs and media coverage.                                    Advisory Board
The journal provides a forum for very different points of view                             James A. Garrison, Jr.
and exposes the reader to the complex nature of relations                                    James L. Hickman
between the Soviet and American governments and between                                       William G. Miller
                                                                                           Stephen H. Rhinesmith
the peoples. The focus of Surviving Together is on construc-                                   Yale Richmond
tive activities, constructive criticism and constructive alter-                                 Mark Satin
natives to an adversarial relationship.                                                      Edward F. Snyder
    Although the journal includes official agreements in its                                     Advisors
coverage, the emphasis is on the wealth of private sector                                      Anne Bartley
initiatives which breathe new life into the bilateral relation-                                 Sarah Carey
                                                                                            Margaret Chapman
ship. We believe that the individual can improve Soviet-                                       Mark Garrison
American relations by using his or her creativity, ingenuity                                 Toby Trister Gati
and resourcefulness to create new exchanges and channels of                                    Tom Greening
                                                                                                Steven Kull
communication. We see these private sector innovations                                        Cynthia Lazaroff
affecting the overall climate of superpower relations, bring-                                 Dennis Meadows
ing new ideas and fresh approaches into government-to-gov-                                  Catherine Menninger
ernment affairs.                                                                              Michael Murphy
                                                                                             John Nicolopolous
                                                                                               Grant Pendill

                                      '11E.IJ E.Lt.C.TJON.    Environmental Worry Up
                                                                   Public support for greater environmental ef-
                                                              forts regardless of cost has soared since the Exxon
                                                              Valdez oil spill in Alaska, a New York Times Poll
                                                              shows. Eighty percent of the 1,497 people inter-
                                                              viewed by telephone from June 20 through 25 said
                                                              they agreed with this statement: "Protecting the
                                                              environment is so important that requirements
                                                              and standards cannot be too high, and continuing
                                                              environmental improvements must be made re-
                                                              gardless of cost." Fourteen percent said they
                                                              disagreed. The poll had a margin of sampling
                                                              error of plus or minus three percentage points.
                                                                   That statement, asked in New York Times/
                                                              CBS News Polls since 1981 in an effort to measure
                                                              unqualified environmental support, had won the
                                                              agreement of 74 percent of the public in April,
                                                              shortly after the spill. From 1986 to 1988 polls had
                                                              recorded ratings in the middle 60 percent range.
                                                              In September 1981, early in the Reagan Admini-
                                                               stration, 45 percent agreed and 42 percent
                                                              disagreed. [New York Times, 4/2/89]

          In summar.v, itappe~s to me that whatever reasons there may once havebeenfor regarding the Soviet Uni.on
      primarily as a po88ihle, ifnot prof,~ble, military opponent, the time for that sort ofth~ng has, clearly pa8sed. That
      coun'try shoulJ. now be regarded essentially as another great power like other greatpowers-one, that is, whp8e
      aspirations and policies are conditioned outstandingly by its own geographic situation, history, and trculition,
      and are.; therefortf!, not identica.lwith our own, but are also not so seriousl1 in conflict with ours a.s tojusti.fy any
      aBBumption that the outstanding differences could not be adjusted by the normal means·of compromise and
      accommodation.
          It ought now.to be our purpose, I consider, while not neglecting the needs ofourgeneral security, to eliminate,
      as soon as possible, by amicable negotiation, the elements of abnormal military tension that have recently
      domina'ted Soviet-American relations, and to turn our attention.1 instead, 'lo the development of the positive
      possibilities ofthis relationship, which are far from insignificant.
                                                                                            AmbassadorGeorge·F. Kennan

Gorbachev Welcomes
Bush Troop Plan
     On June 12 Soviet President
Mikhail Gorbachev welcomed "with
satisfaction" President Bush's recent
initiative to slash troops in Europe
and said the new Western position
should make it possible to reach an
East-West agreement on reducing
conventional forces "much earlier
than previously thought."
     In his first response to the US
proposals, unveiled by Bush two
weeks ago, Gorbachev said at a state
dinner on the first day of an official
visit to West Germany that Bush's
initiative was a serious and specific
response" to Warsaw Pact positions.             Wasserman C1989 TM Boston Globe. Reprinled '1.ith permission or L.A. Times Syndicate.

                                                                   Women Respond
                                                                   To Ecological Crisis
                                                                       At the First International Conference on
                                                                   Women, Peace and the Environment, convened in
                                                                   June in Moscow, 104 women from 24 nations
                                                                   assembled to discuss the ecological perils con-
                                                                   fronting today's world. The participants declared
                                                                   their intention to expose the connections between
                                                                   environmental degradation, greed, militarism
                                                                   and technology devoid of human values; insist
                                                                   that human and ecological values take absolute
                                                                   precedence; build trust among peoples and na-
                                                                   tions, and use all available international institu-
                                                                   tions and networks to achieve a common security
                                                                   for the family of earth. [Press Release]
